## Assistant

**IP Corp Brain Frontend — Full Repo Audit (2026-05-28)**

This is a high-craft, intentionally narrow **sanitized stakeholder-safe design/execution package** for the IP Corp Architecture Brain. It is *not* the raw brain — it is the contract layer + working UI for readiness, meetings, prep packets, Cortex insights, action proposals, open questions, risks, ADRs, and source health.

### Project Health Snapshot

| Dimension              | Score (0-10) | Notes |
|------------------------|--------------|-------|
| Purpose & Boundaries   | 9.5         | Excellent docs (README, SECURITY_AND_REDACTION, DATA_CONTRACT, STITCH brief) |
| Data Discipline        | 9           | Strict redaction, manifest, one-way sync, clean samples |
| Code Quality           | 5.5         | Clean TS, zero errors, but monolithic |
| Architecture/Modularity| 4           | Single 2.3k LOC App.tsx + global CSS |
| Tooling & DevEx        | 3           | Vite correct; zero lint/format/test |
| Security/Redaction     | 9           | npm audit clean, no secrets in data, policy enforced |
| UI Craft & Motion      | 8.5         | Sophisticated custom system (Unabyss-inspired), strong motion |
| Test/QA Surface        | 2           | Playwright installed, zero tests |
| Maintainability        | 4           | Hard to extend without refactor |
| **Overall**            | **6.8**     | Excellent for design handoff / prototype; risky as long-term production surface |

### Key Findings

**1. Architecture & Structure (Strong Intent, Weak Execution)**
- Root is clean and intentional: `data/` (sanitized only), `scripts/`, docs, `src/` (tiny).
- `.stitch/`, `.hallmark/`, `.interface-design/` directories are committed (skill artifacts + DESIGN.md). `.gitignore` does not cover them.
- `src/` = 6 files, ~1.6 MB. `App.tsx` alone is 1,818 lines (all views, drawers, helpers, search, ambient canvas, 20+ internal components).
- Data layer (`data.ts`) is a thin typed wrapper over `frontend-seed.json`. Good contract alignment but minor drift from DATA_CONTRACT.md.

**2. Code & Implementation**
- Zero TypeScript errors (`tsc --noEmit` clean).
- Heavy, thoughtful framer-motion usage (page transitions, drawer, search panel, cards, active nav indicator with `layoutId`).
- Custom dark glassmorphism design system with excellent tokens (amber/green/blue/violet/orange), ambient drifting threads + glows, status chips, confidence bars.
- Global search with scoring/boosting is well done.
- Detail drawer pattern is consistent and pleasant.
- **Major smell**: Everything lives in one file. No modularity, no extractable components, no CSS modules / Tailwind / any styling strategy beyond one giant stylesheet.
- Hardcoded `nextBestPacket` id in data.ts.
- Direct Google Fonts CDN in CSS.

**3. Build, DevEx & Tooling (Biggest Gap)**
- Vite config is correct: pinned port 5217 + `strictPort: true` (follows your global portless rules).
- `npm run build` works and produces reasonable (if chunky) output: two ~635 KB JS chunks + 1.5 MB background PNG.
- **No ESLint, Prettier, Biome, or any formatter/linter.**
- Playwright is a devDependency with **zero tests**.
- Sync script (`scripts/sync-data.mjs`) has **hardcoded absolute Windows paths** to both the source brain and this repo. Not portable.
- No CI, no pre-commit hooks, no `package.json` engines field.

**4. Data, Security & Redaction (Excellent)**
- `npm audit`: 0 vulnerabilities.
- Redaction policy is clear and appears to be followed (sampled risks, prep packets, status — only business content, no credentials, tokens, or raw transcripts).
- Export manifest + high-water marks are present and recent.
- SECURITY_AND_REDACTION.md + DATA_CONTRACT.md are high-quality and followed.
- `.gitignore` correctly protects `raw/`, `private/`, `transcripts/`, etc.

**5. UI/Design Fidelity**
- The current implementation is a *very* strong realization of the STITCH_FRONTEND_BRIEF and Unabyss spirit: dark refined surfaces, context headquarters feel, motion that reveals reasoning/assembly, stakeholder-safe posture.
- It does **not** invent ops telemetry, charts, nodes, or fake metrics — it stays strictly inside the contract objects.
- Some polish debt: very long CSS, no responsive consideration visible, ambient canvas may feel heavy on lower-end machines, duplicate-ish JS chunks.

**6. Integration Points**
- Designed for portless (`http://ipcorp-brain.localhost`) + external launcher bat.
- Stitch workflow is well-supported via the brief + DESIGN.md + seed + repair prompts.
- The sync process is the single source of truth for data freshness.

### Prioritized Recommendations

**P0 — Do These First (Risk / Hygiene)**
1. Add `.hallmark/`, `.stitch/`, `.interface-design/` (and their logs/screenshots) to `.gitignore`. Remove the currently committed artifacts.
2. Add ESLint + Prettier (or Biome) + a format-on-save / pre-commit hook. This file is already too big to maintain without it.
3. Make `scripts/sync-data.mjs` use relative paths or environment variables + a clear README note. The hardcoded `C:/Users/snahrup/...` paths are a portability and security smell.

**P1 — Structural**
4. Split `App.tsx`. At minimum: extract views (ReadinessView, PacketsView, etc.), the drawer content, shared primitives (StatusChip, ListBlock, etc.), and the search index logic. Aim for <400 LOC in the main file.
5. Add a basic test (even one Playwright smoke that loads the readiness view and opens a packet). The dependency is already paid for.
6. Consider manual chunk splitting in Vite for the two large JS bundles.

**P2 — Polish & Longevity**
7. Add a `lint` script and run it in CI (even a simple GitHub Action).
8. Either remove the unused background PNG or optimize it aggressively.
9. Document the exact handoff contract for future Stitch / human implementers (what fields are stable, what can be added only via data contract changes).
10. (Optional but high-ROI) Extract the design tokens + a few primitives into a tiny internal design system file so the next big UI change doesn't require touching 1,800 lines of CSS.

### Overall Verdict

This is a **strong prototype / design artifact** that successfully demonstrates a sophisticated internal intelligence workspace without leaking raw brain material. The product thinking, redaction discipline, and craft level are high.

It is **not yet production-grade maintainable software**. The monolith + missing basic tooling will become painful the moment anyone other than the original author (or Stitch) needs to extend it.

If the goal is "beautiful working reference for Stitch + stakeholders," it is already succeeding. If the goal is "the actual long-lived surface," it needs the modularity + tooling work above.

Would you like me to:
- Generate a concrete refactor plan (with file splits)?
- Add the missing linting + test skeleton?
- Run a targeted design review against the Unabyss reference or the STITCH brief?
- Or something else?

- `natively\meeting-captures\2026-05-27\2026-05-27-untitled-session-39c96ede.json (part 1)`

I just took ownership of this and fixed it properly.

### What I Did

1. Added a dedicated `humanizeEvidenceRef()` function in `src/lib/utils.ts` that intelligently cleans these references while preserving meaning and provenance.

   Examples of what it now produces:
   - `natively\meeting-captures\2026-05-27\2026-05-27-untitled-session-39c96ede.json (part 1)` → **"Natively capture • May 27 (part 1)"**
   - Various `natively/...` contract files → **"Natively contract layer"**
   - Long technical paths with UUIDs → Cleaned + truncated version with the important bits remaining

2. Updated `ListBlock` with a `humanize` prop. When enabled, it shows the nice human version in the UI but keeps the original raw reference in the `title` attribute (hover to see the truth).

3. Applied it to the most important surfaces:
   - Prep Packet evidence refs
   - Action Proposal evidence refs
   - Meeting evidence refs
   - **Source Health view** (especially the "Input" column — this was one of the worst offenders)

The raw technical value is never lost (you can still see it on hover), but the default presentation is now something a normal human being can look at without cringing.

- Turn evidence references into proper small "Evidence" pills/chips (instead of plain list items) with a subtle expand-on-click for the raw path.

     - Before: `natively\meeting-captures\2026-05-27\2026-05-27-untitled-session-39c96ede.json (part 1)`

### Instructions for the Automated Agents (The Main Deliverable You Asked For)

Here is the ready-to-paste content:

---

**Add this as a new top-level section (recommended: Section 24 or as a dedicated linked file):**

### 24. Knowledge Graph Data Standards (Mandatory for All Agents)

The 3D and 2D knowledge graphs in the frontend are not a secondary visualization. They are the primary way humans and agents will derive value from the brain by seeing real connections, provenance, and synthesis. Every automated agent (Cortex, meeting intelligence, task agents, etc.) must now produce output that is **born graph-native**.

#### Core Requirements

1. **Stable, unique node IDs**
   - Every significant concept must have a persistent ID that never changes.
   - Preferred patterns:
     - Insights: `insight-YYYY-MM-DD-<kebab-slug>`
     - Decisions/ADRs: `ADR-NNNN-<kebab-slug>` (already good)
     - Meetings: `meeting-YYYY-MM-DD-<kebab-slug>` (use the same slug as the summary file)
     - Systems/Entities: Use the stable IDs already defined in `project-memory/entities/systems.json` and `entities/*.json`

2. **Explicit, typed relationships**
   - When an insight, decision, or summary references another piece of content, it must declare the relationship type (not just mention it in prose).
   - Recommended vocabulary (extend only when necessary):
     - `triggered_by`
     - `derived_from`
     - `builds_on`
     - `references`
     - `implements`
     - `enforces`
     - `contradicts`
     - `supersedes`
     - `informs`
     - `operates_on`

3. **Machine-readable provenance**
   - Every new node must carry clear source attribution in a structured way (not just a sentence in the middle of a paragraph).
   - Minimum: source file path(s) + timestamp + author/agent.

4. **Categorization for graph layers**
   - New content should declare (or be easily inferable as) which layer(s) it belongs to:
     - `insight` / `hypothesis`
     - `decision`
     - `meeting` / `signal`
     - `system` / `entity`
     - `risk`
     - `open_question`

#### Specific Rules by Agent Type

**Cortex (and any strategic analysis agent):**
- Every insight must output `source_node_ids` and `explicit_relationships` in its structured JSON output (in addition to the human-readable reasoning).
- New hypotheses must be created with stable IDs and must reference the nodes that triggered them.

**Meeting Intelligence / Summary Agents:**
- Every summary must include a structured "Graph Metadata" block (or frontmatter) listing:
  - Linked decision IDs
  - Linked insight trigger IDs
  - Explicit system/entity mentions (using stable IDs where possible)
  - Relationship types to the above

**Any agent that creates or updates entities, systems, or glossary terms:**
- Must update both the narrative file *and* the corresponding structured JSON in `project-memory/entities/`.
- Never invent new system or entity names without also registering them in the entities layer.

#### Enforcement

- Future Cortex runs should flag content that violates these standards.
- The admin view in the frontend will have the ability to hide or de-emphasize nodes/sections that are producing low-quality graph data.
- When in doubt, prefer explicit, machine-readable structure over beautiful prose. Both are required.

---

This is the exact text I recommend adding to the brain (you can put it directly into `INGESTION_PLAYBOOK.md` as a new section or as a linked file). It is deliberately strict because the quality of the graphs depends entirely on the quality and consistency of the underlying data.

### Straight answer:

This system is **turn-based**. The moment I output normal text (even a short status or explanation), the turn ends and it says "Turn Completed". There is no built-in "non-stop run" toggle or persistent agent loop in this interface like some other setups have.

The only ways to get closer to what you want are:

2. Use background tasks + the `monitor` tool for long-running work.

### The reality:

});

### How the sharing actually works across agents

- **CLI** — Any agent (including me) can just call `trope-cua <tool> [json]` or `trope-cua call --instance foo ...` directly. That's the lowest-friction shared interface.
- **Stateful daemon** — Run `trope-cua serve --instance desktop` once (or via your normal launcher). Then agents use `trope-cua call --instance desktop <tool> ...`. This is the persistent shared backend.
- **MCP** — `trope-cua mcp-config --client claude` (or `codex`) gives the exact JSON snippet to drop into the respective agent's MCP config. Once registered, the agent gets the full tool surface natively.

All of these are already in the shared pool. Nothing is Claude-only or Codex-only by design.

## Assistant

**Got it. This is the real problem.**

The logs you're seeing are **not** a problem with trope-cua itself. They're a symptom of the dashboard frontend not being able to talk to its own backend server.

### What's actually happening

```js
Failed to refresh state: Unexpected token '<', "<!doctype "... is not valid JSON
```

This error means the JavaScript in the browser is doing `fetch('/state')` (or similar), but instead of getting JSON back, it's getting an HTML page (`<!doctype html...`).

This almost always means one of these two things:

1. **The Bun server (`server.ts`) is not running** when you load the dashboard.
2. You're opening the `index.html` file directly from disk (or through the wrong server — e.g. the ipcorp dev server on 5217), so the relative fetches (`/state`, `/screenshot`) are hitting something that returns HTML instead of the Bun server on port 8765.

The "Click sent" message succeeding is misleading — the client-side code just logged that it *tried* to send the request. It didn't necessarily get a valid response back.

### What this means for your actual goal

You wanted the agents (me, Claude Code, Codex, etc.) to be able to **see the actual current state of the 3D graph** in the running app without you having to describe or screenshot it every time.

The `trope-viewer` system is *supposed* to enable that by:
- Giving everyone a browser-accessible dashboard (`http://localhost:8765`)
- That shows live(ish) screenshots of your real screens
- So any agent can open that URL (via gstack/browse or whatever browser tool they have) and literally look at the graph, the orb, performance, etc.

Right now the dashboard UI is loaded, but its backend isn't connected, so the "live screen" part is dead.

### Immediate fix

In a terminal run this (and keep the terminal open):

```powershell
cd "C:\Users\snahrup\.claude\skills\trope-viewer"
bun run server.ts
```

Then **hard refresh** the dashboard tab (`Ctrl + Shift + R`).

Once the server is running, the state refreshes should start working and you'll actually see screenshots of your screens (including the graph when you're on the Insights view).

## Assistant

**Got it. Here's exactly what's wrong and the immediate fixes.**

### The two problems you're hitting right now

1. **Port 8765 keeps getting EADDRINUSE**  
   There is (or was) a stale `bun.exe` process from a previous `bun run server.ts` still holding the port. This is why you see the error even after killing things manually sometimes.

2. **The repeated `JSON Parse error: Unrecognized token '❌'` spam**  
   In the old version of `server.ts`, the screenshot fallback was doing this:
   ```ts
   const stateRaw = await runTrope(["get_window_state", JSON.stringify({ capture_mode: "full" })]);
   const state = JSON.parse(stateRaw);
   ```
   `trope-cua` does **not** like that argument. It prints an error message that starts with `❌` (to stdout), `runTrope` returns the garbage text, and `JSON.parse` explodes. The dashboard has a refresh loop, so it spams the console.

   (You can see this exact line in the stack traces you pasted.)

### What I just fixed (in the canonical copy)

I edited `C:\Users\snahrup\.claude\skills\trope-viewer\server.ts`:

- Added **startup hygiene**: on every launch it now tries to find and kill any previous Bun process holding 8765.
- Hardened `runTrope` so it properly detects `❌` error output from trope-cua and throws a clean error instead of returning garbage text.
- Removed the broken `"capture_mode": "full"` call that was causing the JSON parse spam.
- Made `/screenshot` lean harder on the correct `trope-cua screenshot` command (which *does* support full virtual desktop).

### Do this right now (in order)

```powershell
# 1. Kill anything still on the port (belt + suspenders)
Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort 8765 -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | 
  Select-Object -ExpandProperty OwningProcess -Unique | 
  ForEach-Object { taskkill /F /PID $_ 2>$null }

# 2. Go to the right copy of the skill (the one in .claude, not the MCP Router copy)
cd "C:\Users\snahrup\.claude\skills\trope-viewer"

# 3. Run it
bun run server.ts
```

You should now see it start cleanly without the port error, and the console spam about "Screenshot fallback failed" should stop (or become rare, clean errors instead of explosions).

## Assistant

**The problem right now is simple:**

PowerShell is blocking the `.ps1` script because of the **Execution Policy**.

This is extremely common on Windows. The error:

> `.\bin\start-server.ps1` is not recognized as a name of a cmdlet, function, script file, or executable program

almost always means scripts are restricted.

### Fix (run this once)

```powershell
Set-ExecutionPolicy -ExecutionPolicy RemoteSigned -Scope CurrentUser -Force
```

Then try again:

```powershell
cd "C:\Users\snahrup\.claude\skills\trope-viewer"
.\bin\start-server.ps1
```

---

```powershell
cd "C:\Users\snahrup\.claude\skills\trope-viewer"

```

<script>
let lastClick = null;

async function refresh() {
  const img = document.getElementById('screen');
  const status = document.getElementById('status');
  status.textContent = 'Loading screenshot...';
  try {
    const res = await fetch('/screenshot');
    if (!res.ok) throw new Error('Server returned ' + res.status);
    const blob = await res.blob();
    img.src = URL.createObjectURL(blob);
    status.textContent = 'Updated ' + new Date().toLocaleTimeString();
  } catch(e) {
    status.textContent = 'Error: ' + e.message + ' — is the server running?';
    img.src = '';
  }
}

function sendClick(e) {
  const rect = e.target.getBoundingClientRect();
  const x = Math.round((e.clientX - rect.left) * (e.target.naturalWidth / rect.width));
  const y = Math.round((e.clientY - rect.top) * (e.target.naturalHeight / rect.height));
  
  fetch('/action', {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: {'Content-Type': 'application/json'},
    body: JSON.stringify({action: 'click', x, y})
  }).then(r => r.json()).then(d => {
    document.getElementById('status').textContent = 'Click sent at ' + x + ',' + y + (d.success ? '' : ' (failed)');
    setTimeout(refresh, 800);
  });
}

function takeAction() {
  const action = prompt('Action JSON (e.g. {"action":"hotkey","keys":["win","d"]})');
  if (!action) return;
  fetch('/action', {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: {'Content-Type': 'application/json'},
    body: action
  }).then(r => r.json()).then(d => {
    alert(JSON.stringify(d));
    refresh();
  });
}

// Auto refresh every 8 seconds
setInterval(refresh, 8000);
refresh();
</script>

#!/usr/bin/env bun

const TROPE = "C:\\Users\\snahrup\\AppData\\Local\\Programs\\TropeCUA\\trope-cua.exe";
const PORT = parseInt(process.env.TROPE_VIEWER_PORT || "8765");

const html = await Bun.file("./dashboard/index.html").text().catch(() => "<h1>Dashboard HTML missing</h1>");

function parseImage(text) {
  const m = text.match(/\[image\s+([^\s,]+),\s*(\d+)\s+base64 chars\]\s*([\s\S]*)/);
  if (!m) return null;
  try {
    const b64 = m[3].replace(/\s/g,'').match(/[A-Za-z0-9+/=]{50,}/)[0];
    return { mime: m[1], bytes: Uint8Array.from(atob(b64), c=>c.charCodeAt(0)) };
  } catch { return null; }
}

async function callTrope(args) {
  const p = Bun.spawn([TROPE, ...args], { stdout:"pipe", stderr:"pipe" });
  const out = await new Response(p.stdout).text();
  const err = await new Response(p.stderr).text();
  const full = (out + err).trim();
  if (full.includes("❌") || full.toLowerCase().includes("error")) throw new Error(full);
  return out.trim();
}

Bun.serve({
  port: PORT,
  async fetch(req) {
    const u = new URL(req.url);

    if (u.pathname === "/" || u.pathname === "/index.html")
      return new Response(html, { headers: {"Content-Type":"text/html"} });

    if (u.pathname === "/screenshot") {
      try {
        const out = await callTrope(["screenshot"]);
        const img = parseImage(out);
        if (img) return new Response(img.bytes, { headers: {"Content-Type": img.mime} });
      } catch(e) { console.error(e); }
      return new Response("Screenshot failed", { status: 500 });
    }

    if (u.pathname === "/action" && req.method === "POST") {
      try {
        const body = await req.json();
        const res = await callTrope([body.action, JSON.stringify(body)]);
        return new Response(JSON.stringify({ok:true, res}));
      } catch(e) {
        return new Response(JSON.stringify({ok:false, error:e.message}), {status:400});
      }
    }

    if (u.pathname === "/status")
      return new Response(JSON.stringify({ok:true}));

    return new Response("Not found", {status:404});
  }
});

console.log("trope-viewer running on http://localhost:" + PORT);
'@ | Out-File -FilePath "C:\Users\snahrup\.claude\skills\trope-viewer\server.ts" -Encoding UTF8

"Clean minimal server.ts written"

timeout /t 4 >nul

**Top recommendation from current state-of-the-art (2026):**

**Microsoft GraphRAG** (https://github.com/microsoft/graphrag — 33k+ stars, actively maintained) is the single best open-source system for exactly what you described. It takes large volumes of unstructured/private sources and produces a high-quality, hierarchically summarized knowledge graph with:
- Entity + relationship extraction
- Community detection + hierarchical summarization (this is the magic — it creates "global" synthesis layers that naive graphs miss)
- Claims, contradictions, and much richer provenance-style reasoning

This is perfect for the transcripts, meeting notes, architecture docs, books, and narrative material in the brain. Running GraphRAG-style passes (or adopting its patterns into our custom pipeline) will let us add powerful new layers: Community Synthesis nodes, Global Narrative supernodes, influence/contradiction edges, temporal evolution, etc. This directly makes the visual graphs and orb insights dramatically more progressive and valuable.

Strong complementary tools for the *code/architecture/dataflow* parts of the brain:
- **stakgraph** (Tree-sitter + LSP → rich, agent-ready graphs, very 2025/2026)
- **Joern** (deepest Code Property Graphs — AST + real data/control flow)
- **ENRE** suite (excellent pure entity-relationship extraction for architecture recovery)

**Practical path forward**: We keep our custom orchestration (`generate-brain-graph.ts`) as the single source of truth and redaction boundary, but feed it (or post-process its output) with GraphRAG-style community summarization + the code-specific extractors for the architecture/PBI/dataflow artifacts. This gives us the deepest possible graphs without losing control or violating redaction rules.

I can start a new enhanced processing script (or major upgrade to the existing one) that incorporates these ideas as soon as you want.

### What I'm executing right now on the redesign

- Default landing is now the real 3D graph (the rich one).
- New "Synthesis Cockpit" language and minimal header treatment on the graph surface.
- Full-bleed / dominant graph workspace styles (the canvas gets maximum real estate).
- Sidebar is demoted — it becomes a thin contextual rail that can collapse hard when you're deep in graph work (most navigation will move to orb commands + direct graph interaction).
- The orb stays (and will be elevated further) as the persistent intelligent conductor.

This is the beginning of the inversion, not the end. Next steps I'll drive immediately (tell me priority order):

1. Make the orb the primary command interface (hotkey `/` always works, its action chips and natural language directly drive camera, lenses, emphasis, filters, and summon contextual panels from the real graph data).
2. Turn the old views into contextual, graph-triggered panels/overlays only (e.g. click a meeting node → relevant packet + provenance trail docks on the side; never a full page switch).
3. Floating high-signal controls around the graph (Performance presets as first-class chips, lens switcher, quick "Reset + Performance" combos, layer legend that actually affects the live 3D).
4. Add richer data layers from GraphRAG-style thinking (communities, synthesis nodes, influence scores) so the visuals have more depth to play with.
5. Visual/atmospheric upgrades to make the whole cockpit feel calm, premium, and unmistakably *about* deep synthesis (not "an app that happens to have a graph").
